 179 |     |     /**
 180 |     |      * @dev Imitates a Solidity `token.transfer(to, value)` call, relaxing the requirement on the return value: the
 181 |     |      * return value is optional (but if data is returned, it must not be false).
 182 |     |      *
 183 |     |      * @param token The token targeted by the call.
 184 |     |      * @param to The recipient of the tokens
 185 |     |      * @param value The amount of token to transfer
 186 |     |      * @param bubble Behavior switch if the transfer call reverts: bubble the revert reason or return a false boolean.
 187 |     |      */
 188 |     |     function _safeTransfer(IERC20 token, address to, uint256 value, bool bubble) private returns (bool success) {
 189 |     |         bytes4 selector = IERC20.transfer.selector;
 190 |     | 
 191 |     |         assembly ("memory-safe") {
 192 |     |             let fmp := mload(0x40)
 193 |     |             mstore(0x00, selector)
 194 |     |             mstore(0x04, and(to, shr(96, not(0))))
 195 |     |             mstore(0x24, value)
 196 |     |             success := call(gas(), token, 0, 0x00, 0x44, 0x00, 0x20)
 197 |     |             // if call success and return is true, all is good.
 198 |     |             // otherwise (not success or return is not true), we need to perform further checks
 199 |     |             if iszero(and(success, eq(mload(0x00), 1))) {
 200 |     |                 // if the call was a failure and bubble is enabled, bubble the error
 201 |     |                 if and(iszero(success), bubble) {
 202 |     |                     returndatacopy(fmp, 0x00, returndatasize())
 203 |     |                     revert(fmp, returndatasize())
 204 |     |                 }
 205 |     |                 // if the return value is not true, then the call is only successful if:
 206 |     |                 // - the token address has code
 207 |     |                 // - the returndata is empty
 208 |     |                 success := and(success, and(iszero(returndatasize()), gt(extcodesize(token), 0)))
 209 |     |             }
 210 |     |             mstore(0x40, fmp)
 211 |     |         }
 212 |     |     }
 213 |     | 
 214 |     |     /**
 215 |     |      * @dev Imitates a Solidity `token.transferFrom(from, to, value)` call, relaxing the requirement on the return
 216 |     |      * value: the return value is optional (but if data is returned, it must not be false).
 217 |     |      *
 218 |     |      * @param token The token targeted by the call.
 219 |     |      * @param from The sender of the tokens
 220 |     |      * @param to The recipient of the tokens
 221 |     |      * @param value The amount of token to transfer
 222 |     |      * @param bubble Behavior switch if the transfer call reverts: bubble the revert reason or return a false boolean.
 223 |     |      */
 224 |     |     function _safeTransferFrom(
 225 |     |         IERC20 token,
 226 |     |         address from,
 227 |     |         address to,
 228 |     |         uint256 value,
 229 |     |         bool bubble
 230 |     |     ) private returns (bool success) {
 231 |     |         bytes4 selector = IERC20.transferFrom.selector;
 232 |     | 
 233 |     |         assembly ("memory-safe") {
 234 |     |             let fmp := mload(0x40)
 235 |     |             mstore(0x00, selector)
 236 |     |             mstore(0x04, and(from, shr(96, not(0))))
 237 |     |             mstore(0x24, and(to, shr(96, not(0))))
 238 |     |             mstore(0x44, value)
 239 |     |             success := call(gas(), token, 0, 0x00, 0x64, 0x00, 0x20)
 240 |     |             // if call success and return is true, all is good.
 241 |     |             // otherwise (not success or return is not true), we need to perform further checks
 242 |     |             if iszero(and(success, eq(mload(0x00), 1))) {
 243 |     |                 // if the call was a failure and bubble is enabled, bubble the error
 244 |     |                 if and(iszero(success), bubble) {
 245 |     |                     returndatacopy(fmp, 0x00, returndatasize())
 246 |     |                     revert(fmp, returndatasize())
 247 |     |                 }
 248 |     |                 // if the return value is not true, then the call is only successful if:
 249 |     |                 // - the token address has code
 250 |     |                 // - the returndata is empty
 251 |     |                 success := and(success, and(iszero(returndatasize()), gt(extcodesize(token), 0)))
 252 |     |             }
 253 |     |             mstore(0x40, fmp)
 254 |     |             mstore(0x60, 0)
 255 |     |         }
 256 |     |     }
 257 |     | 
 258 |     |     /**
 259 |     |      * @dev Imitates a Solidity `token.approve(spender, value)` call, relaxing the requirement on the return value:
 260 |     |      * the return value is optional (but if data is returned, it must not be false).
 261 |     |      *
 262 |     |      * @param token The token targeted by the call.
 263 |     |      * @param spender The spender of the tokens
 264 |     |      * @param value The amount of token to approve
 265 |     |      * @param bubble Behavior switch if the approve call reverts: bubble the revert reason or return a false boolean.
 266 |     |      */
 267 |     |     function _safeApprove(IERC20 token, address spender, uint256 value, bool bubble) private returns (bool success) {
 268 |     |         bytes4 selector = IERC20.approve.selector;
 269 |     | 
 270 |     |         assembly ("memory-safe") {
 271 |     |             let fmp := mload(0x40)
 272 |     |             mstore(0x00, selector)
 273 |     |             mstore(0x04, and(spender, shr(96, not(0))))
 274 |     |             mstore(0x24, value)
 275 |     |             success := call(gas(), token, 0, 0x00, 0x44, 0x00, 0x20)
 276 |     |             // if call success and return is true, all is good.
 277 |     |             // otherwise (not success or return is not true), we need to perform further checks
 278 |     |             if iszero(and(success, eq(mload(0x00), 1))) {
 279 |     |                 // if the call was a failure and bubble is enabled, bubble the error
 280 |     |                 if and(iszero(success), bubble) {
 281 |     |                     returndatacopy(fmp, 0x00, returndatasize())
 282 |     |                     revert(fmp, returndatasize())
 283 |     |                 }
 284 |     |                 // if the return value is not true, then the call is only successful if:
 285 |     |                 // - the token address has code
 286 |     |                 // - the returndata is empty
 287 |     |                 success := and(success, and(iszero(returndatasize()), gt(extcodesize(token), 0)))
 288 |     |             }
 289 |     |             mstore(0x40, fmp)
 290 |     |         }
 291 |     |     }

 106 |     |     /**
 107 |     |      * @dev Performs a quick sort of a segment of memory. The segment sorted starts at `begin` (inclusive), and stops
 108 |     |      * at end (exclusive). Sorting follows the `comp` comparator.
 109 |     |      *
 110 |     |      * Invariant: `begin <= end`. This is the case when initially called by {sort} and is preserved in subcalls.
 111 |     |      *
 112 |     |      * IMPORTANT: Memory locations between `begin` and `end` are not validated/zeroed. This function should
 113 |     |      * be used only if the limits are within a memory array.
 114 |     |      */
 115 |     |     function _quickSort(uint256 begin, uint256 end, function(uint256, uint256) pure returns (bool) comp) private pure {
 116 |     |         unchecked {
 117 |     |             while (end - begin > 0x20) {
 118 |     |                 // Use first element as pivot
 119 |     |                 uint256 pivot = _mload(begin);
 120 |     |                 // Position where the pivot should be at the end of the loop
 121 |     |                 uint256 pos = begin;
 122 |     | 
 123 |     |                 for (uint256 it = begin + 0x20; it < end; it += 0x20) {
 124 |     |                     if (comp(_mload(it), pivot)) {
 125 |     |                         // If the value stored at the iterator's position comes before the pivot, we increment the
 126 |     |                         // position of the pivot and move the value there.
 127 |     |                         pos += 0x20;
 128 |     |                         _swap(pos, it);
 129 |     |                     }
 130 |     |                 }
 131 |     | 
 132 |     |                 _swap(begin, pos); // Swap pivot into place
 133 |     | 
 134 |     |                 // Recurse on the smaller partition, iterate on the larger one.
 135 |     |                 uint256 middle = pos + 0x20;
 136 |     |                 if (pos - begin < end - middle) {
 137 |     |                     _quickSort(begin, pos, comp);
 138 |     |                     begin = middle;
 139 |     |                 } else {
 140 |     |                     _quickSort(middle, end, comp);
 141 |     |                     end = pos;
 142 |     |                 }
 143 |     |             }
 144 |     |         }
 145 |     |     }

 219 |     |     /**
 220 |     |      * @dev Searches a sorted `array` and returns the first index that contains
 221 |     |      * a value greater or equal to `element`. If no such index exists (i.e. all
 222 |     |      * values in the array are strictly less than `element`), the array length is
 223 |     |      * returned. Time complexity O(log n).
 224 |     |      *
 225 |     |      * NOTE: The `array` is expected to be sorted in ascending order, and to
 226 |     |      * contain no repeated elements.
 227 |     |      *
 228 |     |      * IMPORTANT: Deprecated. This implementation behaves as {lowerBound} but lacks
 229 |     |      * support for repeated elements in the array. The {lowerBound} function should
 230 |     |      * be used instead.
 231 |     |      */
 232 |     |     function findUpperBound(uint256[] storage array, uint256 element) internal view returns (uint256) {
 233 |     |         uint256 low = 0;
 234 |     |         uint256 high = array.length;
 235 |     | 
 236 |     |         if (high == 0) {
 237 |     |             return 0;
 238 |     |         }
 239 |     | 
 240 |     |         while (low < high) {
 241 |     |             uint256 mid = Math.average(low, high);
 242 |     | 
 243 |     |             // Note that mid will always be strictly less than high (i.e. it will be a valid array index)
 244 |     |             // because Math.average rounds towards zero (it does integer division with truncation).
 245 |     |             if (unsafeAccess(array, mid).value > element) {
 246 |     |                 high = mid;
 247 |     |             } else {
 248 |     |                 low = mid + 1;
 249 |     |             }
 250 |     |         }
 251 |     | 
 252 |     |         // At this point `low` is the exclusive upper bound. We will return the inclusive upper bound.
 253 |     |         if (low > 0 && unsafeAccess(array, low - 1).value == element) {
 254 |     |             return low - 1;
 255 |     |         } else {
 256 |     |             return low;
 257 |     |         }
 258 |     |     }
 259 |     | 
 260 |     |     /**
 261 |     |      * @dev Searches an `array` sorted in ascending order and returns the first
 262 |     |      * index that contains a value greater or equal than `element`. If no such index
 263 |     |      * exists (i.e. all values in the array are strictly less than `element`), the array
 264 |     |      * length is returned. Time complexity O(log n).
 265 |     |      *
 266 |     |      * See C++'s https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/algorithm/lower_bound[lower_bound].
 267 |     |      */
 268 |     |     function lowerBound(uint256[] storage array, uint256 element) internal view returns (uint256) {
 269 |     |         uint256 low = 0;
 270 |     |         uint256 high = array.length;
 271 |     | 
 272 |     |         if (high == 0) {
 273 |     |             return 0;
 274 |     |         }
 275 |     | 
 276 |     |         while (low < high) {
 277 |     |             uint256 mid = Math.average(low, high);
 278 |     | 
 279 |     |             // Note that mid will always be strictly less than high (i.e. it will be a valid array index)
 280 |     |             // because Math.average rounds towards zero (it does integer division with truncation).
 281 |     |             if (unsafeAccess(array, mid).value < element) {
 282 |     |                 // this cannot overflow because mid < high
 283 |     |                 unchecked {
 284 |     |                     low = mid + 1;
 285 |     |                 }
 286 |     |             } else {
 287 |     |                 high = mid;
 288 |     |             }
 289 |     |         }
 290 |     | 
 291 |     |         return low;
 292 |     |     }
 293 |     | 
 294 |     |     /**
 295 |     |      * @dev Searches an `array` sorted in ascending order and returns the first
 296 |     |      * index that contains a value strictly greater than `element`. If no such index
 297 |     |      * exists (i.e. all values in the array are strictly less than `element`), the array
 298 |     |      * length is returned. Time complexity O(log n).
 299 |     |      *
 300 |     |      * See C++'s https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/algorithm/upper_bound[upper_bound].
 301 |     |      */
 302 |     |     function upperBound(uint256[] storage array, uint256 element) internal view returns (uint256) {
 303 |     |         uint256 low = 0;
 304 |     |         uint256 high = array.length;
 305 |     | 
 306 |     |         if (high == 0) {
 307 |     |             return 0;
 308 |     |         }
 309 |     | 
 310 |     |         while (low < high) {
 311 |     |             uint256 mid = Math.average(low, high);
 312 |     | 
 313 |     |             // Note that mid will always be strictly less than high (i.e. it will be a valid array index)
 314 |     |             // because Math.average rounds towards zero (it does integer division with truncation).
 315 |     |             if (unsafeAccess(array, mid).value > element) {
 316 |     |                 high = mid;
 317 |     |             } else {
 318 |     |                 // this cannot overflow because mid < high
 319 |     |                 unchecked {
 320 |     |                     low = mid + 1;
 321 |     |                 }
 322 |     |             }
 323 |     |         }
 324 |     | 
 325 |     |         return low;
 326 |     |     }
 327 |     | 
 328 |     |     /**
 329 |     |      * @dev Same as {lowerBound}, but with an array in memory.
 330 |     |      */
 331 |     |     function lowerBoundMemory(uint256[] memory array, uint256 element) internal pure returns (uint256) {
 332 |     |         uint256 low = 0;
 333 |     |         uint256 high = array.length;
 334 |     | 
 335 |     |         if (high == 0) {
 336 |     |             return 0;
 337 |     |         }
 338 |     | 
 339 |     |         while (low < high) {
 340 |     |             uint256 mid = Math.average(low, high);
 341 |     | 
 342 |     |             // Note that mid will always be strictly less than high (i.e. it will be a valid array index)
 343 |     |             // because Math.average rounds towards zero (it does integer division with truncation).
 344 |     |             if (unsafeMemoryAccess(array, mid) < element) {
 345 |     |                 // this cannot overflow because mid < high
 346 |     |                 unchecked {
 347 |     |                     low = mid + 1;
 348 |     |                 }
 349 |     |             } else {
 350 |     |                 high = mid;
 351 |     |             }
 352 |     |         }
 353 |     | 
 354 |     |         return low;
 355 |     |     }
 356 |     | 
 357 |     |     /**
 358 |     |      * @dev Same as {upperBound}, but with an array in memory.
 359 |     |      */
 360 |     |     function upperBoundMemory(uint256[] memory array, uint256 element) internal pure returns (uint256) {
 361 |     |         uint256 low = 0;
 362 |     |         uint256 high = array.length;
 363 |     | 
 364 |     |         if (high == 0) {
 365 |     |             return 0;
 366 |     |         }
 367 |     | 
 368 |     |         while (low < high) {
 369 |     |             uint256 mid = Math.average(low, high);
 370 |     | 
 371 |     |             // Note that mid will always be strictly less than high (i.e. it will be a valid array index)
 372 |     |             // because Math.average rounds towards zero (it does integer division with truncation).
 373 |     |             if (unsafeMemoryAccess(array, mid) > element) {
 374 |     |                 high = mid;
 375 |     |             } else {
 376 |     |                 // this cannot overflow because mid < high
 377 |     |                 unchecked {
 378 |     |                     low = mid + 1;
 379 |     |                 }
 380 |     |             }
 381 |     |         }
 382 |     | 
 383 |     |         return low;
 384 |     |     }
 385 |     | 
 386 |     |     /**
 387 |     |      * @dev Copies the content of `array`, from `start` (included) to the end of `array` into a new address array in
 388 |     |      * memory.
 389 |     |      *
 390 |     |      * NOTE: replicates the behavior of https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/JavaScript/Reference/Global_Objects/Array/slice[Javascript's `Array.slice`]
 391 |     |      */
 392 |     |     function slice(address[] memory array, uint256 start) internal pure returns (address[] memory) {
 393 |     |         return slice(array, start, array.length);
 394 |     |     }
 395 |     | 
 396 |     |     /**
 397 |     |      * @dev Copies the content of `array`, from `start` (included) to `end` (excluded) into a new address array in
 398 |     |      * memory. The `end` argument is truncated to the length of the `array`.
 399 |     |      *
 400 |     |      * NOTE: replicates the behavior of https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/JavaScript/Reference/Global_Objects/Array/slice[Javascript's `Array.slice`]
 401 |     |      */
 402 |     |     function slice(address[] memory array, uint256 start, uint256 end) internal pure returns (address[] memory) {
 403 |     |         // sanitize
 404 |     |         end = Math.min(end, array.length);
 405 |     |         start = Math.min(start, end);
 406 |     | 
 407 |     |         // allocate and copy
 408 |     |         address[] memory result = new address[](end - start);
 409 |     |         assembly ("memory-safe") {
 410 |     |             mcopy(add(result, 0x20), add(add(array, 0x20), mul(start, 0x20)), mul(sub(end, start), 0x20))
 411 |     |         }
 412 |     | 
 413 |     |         return result;
 414 |     |     }
 415 |     | 
 416 |     |     /**

 418 |     |      * memory.

 420 |     |      * NOTE: replicates the behavior of https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/JavaScript/Reference/Global_Objects/Array/slice[Javascript's `Array.slice`]
 421 |     |      */
 422 |     |     function slice(bytes32[] memory array, uint256 start) internal pure returns (bytes32[] memory) {
 423 |     |         return slice(array, start, array.length);
 424 |     |     }

 430 |     |      * NOTE: replicates the behavior of https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/JavaScript/Reference/Global_Objects/Array/slice[Javascript's `Array.slice`]
 431 |     |      */
 432 |     |     function slice(bytes32[] memory array, uint256 start, uint256 end) internal pure returns (bytes32[] memory) {

 441 |     |         }

 456 |     |     /**
 457 |     |      * @dev Copies the content of `array`, from `start` (included) to `end` (excluded) into a new uint256 array in
 458 |     |      * memory. The `end` argument is truncated to the length of the `array`.
 459 |     |      *
 460 |     |      * NOTE: replicates the behavior of https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/JavaScript/Reference/Global_Objects/Array/slice[Javascript's `Array.slice`]
 461 |     |      */
 462 |     |     function slice(uint256[] memory array, uint256 start, uint256 end) internal pure returns (uint256[] memory) {
 463 |     |         // sanitize
 464 |     |         end = Math.min(end, array.length);
 465 |     |         start = Math.min(start, end);
 466 |     | 
 467 |     |         // allocate and copy
 468 |     |         uint256[] memory result = new uint256[](end - start);
 469 |     |         assembly ("memory-safe") {
 470 |     |             mcopy(add(result, 0x20), add(add(array, 0x20), mul(start, 0x20)), mul(sub(end, start), 0x20))
 471 |     |         }
 472 |     | 
 473 |     |         return result;
 474 |     |     }
 475 |     | 
 476 |     |     /**
 477 |     |      * @dev Moves the content of `array`, from `start` (included) to the end of `array` to the start of that array,
 478 |     |      * and shrinks the array length accordingly, effectively overwriting the array with array[start:].
 479 |     |      *
 480 |     |      * NOTE: This function modifies the provided array in place. If you need to preserve the original array, use {slice} instead.
 481 |     |      */
 482 |     |     function splice(address[] memory array, uint256 start) internal pure returns (address[] memory) {
 483 |     |         return splice(array, start, array.length);
 484 |     |     }
 485 |     | 
 486 |     |     /**
 487 |     |      * @dev Moves the content of `array`, from `start` (included) to `end` (excluded) to the start of that array,
 488 |     |      * and shrinks the array length accordingly, effectively overwriting the array with array[start:end]. The
 489 |     |      * `end` argument is truncated to the length of the `array`.
 490 |     |      *
 491 |     |      * NOTE: This function modifies the provided array in place. If you need to preserve the original array, use {slice} instead.
 492 |     |      */
 493 |     |     function splice(address[] memory array, uint256 start, uint256 end) internal pure returns (address[] memory) {
 494 |     |         // sanitize
 495 |     |         end = Math.min(end, array.length);
 496 |     |         start = Math.min(start, end);
 497 |     | 
 498 |     |         // move and resize
 499 |     |         assembly ("memory-safe") {
 500 |     |             mcopy(add(array, 0x20), add(add(array, 0x20), mul(start, 0x20)), mul(sub(end, start), 0x20))
 501 |     |             mstore(array, sub(end, start))
 502 |     |         }
 503 |     | 
 504 |     |         return array;
 505 |     |     }
 506 |     | 
 507 |     |     /**
 508 |     |      * @dev Replaces elements in `array` starting at `pos` with all elements from `replacement`.
 509 |     |      *
 510 |     |      * Parameters are clamped to valid ranges (e.g. `pos` is clamped to `[0, array.length]`).
 511 |     |      * If `pos >= array.length`, no replacement occurs and the array is returned unchanged.
 512 |     |      *
 513 |     |      * NOTE: This function modifies the provided array in place.
 514 |     |      */
 515 |     |     function replace(
 516 |     |         address[] memory array,
 517 |     |         uint256 pos,
 518 |     |         address[] memory replacement
 519 |     |     ) internal pure returns (address[] memory) {
 520 |     |         return replace(array, pos, replacement, 0, replacement.length);
 521 |     |     }
 522 |     | 
 523 |     |     /**
 524 |     |      * @dev Replaces elements in `array` starting at `pos` with elements from `replacement` starting at `offset`.
 525 |     |      * Copies at most `length` elements from `replacement` to `array`.
 526 |     |      *
 527 |     |      * Parameters are clamped to valid ranges (i.e. `pos` is clamped to `[0, array.length]`, `offset` is
 528 |     |      * clamped to `[0, replacement.length]`, and `length` is clamped to `min(length, replacement.length - offset,
 529 |     |      * array.length - pos)`). If `pos >= array.length` or `offset >= replacement.length`, no replacement occurs
 530 |     |      * and the array is returned unchanged.
 531 |     |      *
 532 |     |      * NOTE: This function modifies the provided array in place.
 533 |     |      */
 534 |     |     function replace(
 535 |     |         address[] memory array,
 536 |     |         uint256 pos,
 537 |     |         address[] memory replacement,
 538 |     |         uint256 offset,
 539 |     |         uint256 length
 540 |     |     ) internal pure returns (address[] memory) {
 541 |     |         // sanitize
 542 |     |         pos = Math.min(pos, array.length);
 543 |     |         offset = Math.min(offset, replacement.length);
 544 |     |         length = Math.min(length, Math.min(replacement.length - offset, array.length - pos));
 545 |     | 
 546 |     |         // replace
 547 |     |         assembly ("memory-safe") {
 548 |     |             mcopy(
 549 |     |                 add(add(array, 0x20), mul(pos, 0x20)),
 550 |     |                 add(add(replacement, 0x20), mul(offset, 0x20)),
 551 |     |                 mul(length, 0x20)
 552 |     |             )
 553 |     |         }
 554 |     | 
 555 |     |         return array;
 556 |     |     }
 557 |     |

 560 |     |      * and shrinks the array length accordingly, effectively overwriting the array with array[start:].
 561 |     |      *
 562 |     |      * NOTE: This function modifies the provided array in place. If you need to preserve the original array, use {slice} instead.
 563 |     |      */
 564 |     |     function splice(bytes32[] memory array, uint256 start) internal pure returns (bytes32[] memory) {

 815 |     |         assembly {
 816 |     |             res := mload(add(add(arr, 0x20), mul(pos, 0x20)))
 817 |     |         }
 818 |     |     }
 819 |     | 
 820 |     |     /**
 821 |     |      * @dev Access an array in an "unsafe" way. Skips solidity "index-out-of-range" check.
 822 |     |      *
 823 |     |      * WARNING: Only use if you are certain `pos` is lower than the array length.
 824 |     |      */
 825 |     |     function unsafeMemoryAccess(bytes[] memory arr, uint256 pos) internal pure returns (bytes memory res) {
 826 |     |         assembly {
 827 |     |             res := mload(add(add(arr, 0x20), mul(pos, 0x20)))
 828 |     |         }
 829 |     |     }
 830 |     | 
 831 |     |     /**
 832 |     |      * @dev Access an array in an "unsafe" way. Skips solidity "index-out-of-range" check.
 833 |     |      *
 834 |     |      * WARNING: Only use if you are certain `pos` is lower than the array length.
 835 |     |      */
 836 |     |     function unsafeMemoryAccess(string[] memory arr, uint256 pos) internal pure returns (string memory res) {
 837 |     |         assembly {
 838 |     |             res := mload(add(add(arr, 0x20), mul(pos, 0x20)))
 839 |     |         }
 840 |     |     }
 841 |     | 
 842 |     |     /**
 843 |     |      * @dev Helper to set the length of a dynamic array. Directly writing to `.length` is forbidden.
 844 |     |      *
 845 |     |      * WARNING: this does not clear elements if length is reduced, or initialize elements if length is increased.
 846 |     |      */
 847 |     |     function unsafeSetLength(address[] storage array, uint256 len) internal {
 848 |     |         assembly ("memory-safe") {
 849 |     |             sstore(array.slot, len)
 850 |     |         }
 851 |     |     }

/Users/undef1ned/Work/local/cpredict/lib/openzeppelin-contracts/contracts/utils/Create2.sol
  1 |     | // SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
  2 |     | // OpenZeppelin Contracts (last updated v5.5.0) (utils/Create2.sol)
  3 |     | 
  4 |     | pragma solidity ^0.8.20;
  5 |     | 
  6 |     | import {Errors} from "./Errors.sol";
  7 |     | import {LowLevelCall} from "./LowLevelCall.sol";
  8 |     | 
  9 |     | /**
 10 |     |  * @dev Helper to make usage of the `CREATE2` EVM opcode easier and safer.
 11 |     |  * `CREATE2` can be used to compute in advance the address where a smart
 12 |     |  * contract will be deployed, which allows for interesting new mechanisms known
 13 |     |  * as 'counterfactual interactions'.
 14 |     |  *
 15 |     |  * See the https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-1014#motivation[EIP] for more
 16 |     |  * information.
 17 |     |  */
 18 |     | library Create2 {
 19 |     |     /**
 20 |     |      * @dev There's no code to deploy.
 21 |     |      */
 22 |     |     error Create2EmptyBytecode();
 23 |     | 
 24 |     |     /**
 25 |     |      * @dev Deploys a contract using `CREATE2`. The address where the contract
 26 |     |      * will be deployed can be known in advance via {computeAddress}.
 27 |     |      *
 28 |     |      * The bytecode for a contract can be obtained from Solidity with
 29 |     |      * `type(contractName).creationCode`.
 30 |     |      *
 31 |     |      * Requirements:
 32 |     |      *
 33 |     |      * - `bytecode` must not be empty.
 34 |     |      * - `salt` must have not been used for `bytecode` already.
 35 |     |      * - the factory must have a balance of at least `amount`.
 36 |     |      * - if `amount` is non-zero, `bytecode` must have a `payable` constructor.
 37 |     |      */
 38 |     |     function deploy(uint256 amount, bytes32 salt, bytes memory bytecode) internal returns (address addr) {
 39 |     |         if (address(this).balance < amount) {
 40 |     |             revert Errors.InsufficientBalance(address(this).balance, amount);
 41 |     |         }
 42 |     |         if (bytecode.length == 0) {
 43 |     |             revert Create2EmptyBytecode();
 44 |     |         }
 45 |     |         assembly ("memory-safe") {
 46 |     |             addr := create2(amount, add(bytecode, 0x20), mload(bytecode), salt)
 47 |     |         }
 48 |     |         if (addr == address(0)) {
 49 |     |             if (LowLevelCall.returnDataSize() == 0) {
 50 |     |                 revert Errors.FailedDeployment();
 51 |     |             } else {
 52 |     |                 LowLevelCall.bubbleRevert();
 53 |     |             }
 54 |     |         }
 55 |     |     }
 56 |     | 
 57 |     |     /**
 58 |     |      * @dev Returns the address where a contract will be stored if deployed via {deploy}. Any change in the
 59 |     |      * `bytecodeHash` or `salt` will result in a new destination address.
 60 |     |      */
 61 |     |     function computeAddress(bytes32 salt, bytes32 bytecodeHash) internal view returns (address) {
 62 |     |         return computeAddress(salt, bytecodeHash, address(this));
 63 |     |     }
 64 |     | 
 65 |     |     /**
 66 |     |      * @dev Returns the address where a contract will be stored if deployed via {deploy} from a contract located at
 67 |     |      * `deployer`. If `deployer` is this contract's address, returns the same value as {computeAddress}.
 68 |     |      */
 69 |     |     function computeAddress(bytes32 salt, bytes32 bytecodeHash, address deployer) internal pure returns (address addr) {
 70 |     |         assembly ("memory-safe") {
 71 |     |             let ptr := mload(0x40) // Get free memory pointer
 72 |     | 
 73 |     |             // |                     | ↓ ptr ...  ↓ ptr + 0x0B (start) ...  ↓ ptr + 0x20 ...  ↓ ptr + 0x40 ...   |
 74 |     |             // |---------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------|
 75 |     |             // | bytecodeHash        |                                                        CCCCCCCCCCCCC...CC |
 76 |     |             // | salt                |                                      BBBBBBBBBBBBB...BB                   |
 77 |     |             // | deployer            | 000000...0000AAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAA...AA                                     |
 78 |     |             // | 0xFF                |            FF                                                             |
 79 |     |             // |---------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------|
 80 |     |             // | memory              | 000000...00FFAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAA...AABBBBBBBBBBBBB...BBCCCCCCCCCCCCC...CC |
 81 |     |             // | keccak(start, 0x55) |            ↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑↑ |
 82 |     | 
 83 |     |             mstore(add(ptr, 0x40), bytecodeHash)
 84 |     |             mstore(add(ptr, 0x20), salt)
 85 |     |             mstore(ptr, deployer) // Right-aligned with 12 preceding garbage bytes
 86 |     |             let start := add(ptr, 0x0b) // The hashed data starts at the final garbage byte which we will set to 0xff
 87 |     |             mstore8(start, 0xff)
 88 |     |             addr := and(keccak256(start, 0x55), 0xffffffffffffffffffffffffffffffffffffffff)
 89 |     |         }
 90 |     |     }
 91 |     | }
 92 |     |

/Users/undef1ned/Work/local/cpredict/lib/openzeppelin-contracts/contracts/utils/LowLevelCall.sol
   1 |     | // SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
   2 |     | // OpenZeppelin Contracts (last updated v5.6.0) (utils/LowLevelCall.sol)
   3 |     | 
   4 |     | pragma solidity ^0.8.20;
   5 |     | 
   6 |     | /**
   7 |     |  * @dev Library of low level call functions that implement different calling strategies to deal with the return data.
   8 |     |  *
   9 |     |  * WARNING: Using this library requires an advanced understanding of Solidity and how the EVM works. It is recommended
  10 |     |  * to use the {Address} library instead.
  11 |     |  */
  12 |     | library LowLevelCall {
  13 |     |     /// @dev Performs a Solidity function call using a low level `call` and ignoring the return data.
  14 |     |     function callNoReturn(address target, bytes memory data) internal returns (bool success) {
  15 |     |         return callNoReturn(target, 0, data);
  16 |     |     }
  17 |     | 
  18 |     |     /// @dev Same as {callNoReturn-address-bytes}, but allows specifying the value to be sent in the call.
  19 |     |     function callNoReturn(address target, uint256 value, bytes memory data) internal returns (bool success) {
  20 |     |         assembly ("memory-safe") {
  21 |     |             success := call(gas(), target, value, add(data, 0x20), mload(data), 0x00, 0x00)
  22 |     |         }
  23 |     |     }
  24 |     | 
  25 |     |     /// @dev Performs a Solidity function call using a low level `call` and returns the first 64 bytes of the result
  26 |     |     /// in the scratch space of memory. Useful for functions that return a tuple with two single-word values.
  27 |     |     ///
  28 |     |     /// WARNING: Do not assume that the results are zero if `success` is false. Memory can be already allocated
  29 |     |     /// and this function doesn't zero it out.
  30 |     |     function callReturn64Bytes(
  31 |     |         address target,
  32 |     |         bytes memory data
  33 |     |     ) internal returns (bool success, bytes32 result1, bytes32 result2) {
  34 |     |         return callReturn64Bytes(target, 0, data);
  35 |     |     }
  36 |     | 
  37 |     |     /// @dev Same as {callReturn64Bytes-address-bytes}, but allows specifying the value to be sent in the call.
  38 |     |     function callReturn64Bytes(
  39 |     |         address target,
  40 |     |         uint256 value,
  41 |     |         bytes memory data
  42 |     |     ) internal returns (bool success, bytes32 result1, bytes32 result2) {
  43 |     |         assembly ("memory-safe") {
  44 |     |             success := call(gas(), target, value, add(data, 0x20), mload(data), 0x00, 0x40)
  45 |     |             result1 := mload(0x00)
  46 |     |             result2 := mload(0x20)
  47 |     |         }
  48 |     |     }
  49 |     | 
  50 |     |     /// @dev Performs a Solidity function call using a low level `staticcall` and ignoring the return data.
  51 |     |     function staticcallNoReturn(address target, bytes memory data) internal view returns (bool success) {
  52 |     |         assembly ("memory-safe") {
  53 |     |             success := staticcall(gas(), target, add(data, 0x20), mload(data), 0x00, 0x00)
  54 |     |         }
  55 |     |     }
  56 |     | 
  57 |     |     /// @dev Performs a Solidity function call using a low level `staticcall` and returns the first 64 bytes of the result
  58 |     |     /// in the scratch space of memory. Useful for functions that return a tuple with two single-word values.
  59 |     |     ///
  60 |     |     /// WARNING: Do not assume that the results are zero if `success` is false. Memory can be already allocated
  61 |     |     /// and this function doesn't zero it out.
  62 |     |     function staticcallReturn64Bytes(
  63 |     |         address target,
  64 |     |         bytes memory data
  65 |     |     ) internal view returns (bool success, bytes32 result1, bytes32 result2) {
  66 |     |         assembly ("memory-safe") {
  67 |     |             success := staticcall(gas(), target, add(data, 0x20), mload(data), 0x00, 0x40)
  68 |     |             result1 := mload(0x00)
  69 |     |             result2 := mload(0x20)
  70 |     |         }
  71 |     |     }
  72 |     | 
  73 |     |     /// @dev Performs a Solidity function call using a low level `delegatecall` and ignoring the return data.
  74 |     |     function delegatecallNoReturn(address target, bytes memory data) internal returns (bool success) {
  75 |     |         assembly ("memory-safe") {
  76 |     |             success := delegatecall(gas(), target, add(data, 0x20), mload(data), 0x00, 0x00)
  77 |     |         }
  78 |     |     }
  79 |     | 
  80 |     |     /// @dev Performs a Solidity function call using a low level `delegatecall` and returns the first 64 bytes of the result
  81 |     |     /// in the scratch space of memory. Useful for functions that return a tuple with two single-word values.
  82 |     |     ///
  83 |     |     /// WARNING: Do not assume that the results are zero if `success` is false. Memory can be already allocated
  84 |     |     /// and this function doesn't zero it out.
  85 |     |     function delegatecallReturn64Bytes(
  86 |     |         address target,
  87 |     |         bytes memory data
  88 |     |     ) internal returns (bool success, bytes32 result1, bytes32 result2) {
  89 |     |         assembly ("memory-safe") {
  90 |     |             success := delegatecall(gas(), target, add(data, 0x20), mload(data), 0x00, 0x40)
  91 |     |             result1 := mload(0x00)
  92 |     |             result2 := mload(0x20)
  93 |     |         }
  94 |     |     }
  95 |     | 
  96 |     |     /// @dev Returns the size of the return data buffer.
  97 |     |     function returnDataSize() internal pure returns (uint256 size) {
  98 |     |         assembly ("memory-safe") {
  99 |     |             size := returndatasize()
 100 |     |         }
 101 |     |     }
 102 |     | 
 103 |     |     /// @dev Returns a buffer containing the return data from the last call.
 104 |     |     function returnData() internal pure returns (bytes memory result) {
 105 |     |         assembly ("memory-safe") {
 106 |     |             result := mload(0x40)
 107 |     |             mstore(result, returndatasize())
 108 |     |             returndatacopy(add(result, 0x20), 0x00, returndatasize())
 109 |     |             mstore(0x40, add(result, add(0x20, returndatasize())))
 110 |     |         }
 111 |     |     }
 112 |     | 
 113 |     |     /// @dev Revert with the return data from the last call.
 114 |     |     function bubbleRevert() internal pure {
 115 |     |         assembly ("memory-safe") {
 116 |     |             let fmp := mload(0x40)
 117 |     |             returndatacopy(fmp, 0x00, returndatasize())
 118 |     |             revert(fmp, returndatasize())
 119 |     |         }
 120 |     |     }
 121 |     | 
 122 |     |     function bubbleRevert(bytes memory returndata) internal pure {
 123 |     |         assembly ("memory-safe") {
 124 |     |             revert(add(returndata, 0x20), mload(returndata))
 125 |     |         }
 126 |     |     }
 127 |     | }

/Users/undef1ned/Work/local/cpredict/lib/openzeppelin-contracts/contracts/utils/Panic.sol
  1 |     | // SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
  2 |     | // OpenZeppelin Contracts (last updated v5.1.0) (utils/Panic.sol)
  3 |     | 
  4 |     | pragma solidity ^0.8.20;
  5 |     | 
  6 |     | /**
  7 |     |  * @dev Helper library for emitting standardized panic codes.
  8 |     |  *
  9 |     |  * ```solidity
 10 |     |  * contract Example {
 11 |     |  *      using Panic for uint256;
 12 |     |  *
 13 |     |  *      // Use any of the declared internal constants
 14 |     |  *      function foo() { Panic.GENERIC.panic(); }
 15 |     |  *
 16 |     |  *      // Alternatively
 17 |     |  *      function foo() { Panic.panic(Panic.GENERIC); }
 18 |     |  * }
 19 |     |  * ```
 20 |     |  *
 21 |     |  * Follows the list from https://github.com/ethereum/solidity/blob/v0.8.24/libsolutil/ErrorCodes.h[libsolutil].
 22 |     |  *
 23 |     |  * _Available since v5.1._
 24 |     |  */
 25 |     | // slither-disable-next-line unused-state
 26 |     | library Panic {
 27 |     |     /// @dev generic / unspecified error
 28 |     |     uint256 internal constant GENERIC = 0x00;
 29 |     |     /// @dev used by the assert() builtin
 30 |     |     uint256 internal constant ASSERT = 0x01;
 31 |     |     /// @dev arithmetic underflow or overflow
 32 |     |     uint256 internal constant UNDER_OVERFLOW = 0x11;
 33 |     |     /// @dev division or modulo by zero
 34 |     |     uint256 internal constant DIVISION_BY_ZERO = 0x12;
 35 |     |     /// @dev enum conversion error
 36 |     |     uint256 internal constant ENUM_CONVERSION_ERROR = 0x21;
 37 |     |     /// @dev invalid encoding in storage
 38 |     |     uint256 internal constant STORAGE_ENCODING_ERROR = 0x22;
 39 |     |     /// @dev empty array pop
 40 |     |     uint256 internal constant EMPTY_ARRAY_POP = 0x31;
 41 |     |     /// @dev array out of bounds access
 42 |     |     uint256 internal constant ARRAY_OUT_OF_BOUNDS = 0x32;
 43 |     |     /// @dev resource error (too large allocation or too large array)
 44 |     |     uint256 internal constant RESOURCE_ERROR = 0x41;
 45 |     |     /// @dev calling invalid internal function
 46 |     |     uint256 internal constant INVALID_INTERNAL_FUNCTION = 0x51;
 47 |     | 
 48 |     |     /// @dev Reverts with a panic code. Recommended to use with
 49 |     |     /// the internal constants with predefined codes.
 50 |     |     function panic(uint256 code) internal pure {
 51 |     |         assembly ("memory-safe") {
 52 |     |             mstore(0x00, 0x4e487b71)
 53 |     |             mstore(0x20, code)
 54 |     |             revert(0x1c, 0x24)
 55 |     |         }
 56 |     |     }
 57 |     | }
 58 |     | 

/Users/undef1ned/Work/local/cpredict/lib/openzeppelin-contracts/contracts/utils/ReentrancyGuard.sol
   1 |     | // SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
   2 |     | // OpenZeppelin Contracts (last updated v5.5.0) (utils/ReentrancyGuard.sol)
   3 |     | 
   4 |     | pragma solidity ^0.8.20;
   5 |     | 
   6 |     | import {StorageSlot} from "./StorageSlot.sol";
   7 |     | 
   8 |     | /**
   9 |     |  * @dev Contract module that helps prevent reentrant calls to a function.
  10 |     |  *
  11 |     |  * Inheriting from `ReentrancyGuard` will make the {nonReentrant} modifier
  12 |     |  * available, which can be applied to functions to make sure there are no nested
  13 |     |  * (reentrant) calls to them.
  14 |     |  *
  15 |     |  * Note that because there is a single `nonReentrant` guard, functions marked as
  16 |     |  * `nonReentrant` may not call one another. This can be worked around by making
  17 |     |  * those functions `private`, and then adding `external` `nonReentrant` entry
  18 |     |  * points to them.
  19 |     |  *
  20 |     |  * TIP: If EIP-1153 (transient storage) is available on the chain you're deploying at,
  21 |     |  * consider using {ReentrancyGuardTransient} instead.
  22 |     |  *
  23 |     |  * TIP: If you would like to learn more about reentrancy and alternative ways
  24 |     |  * to protect against it, check out our blog post
  25 |     |  * https://blog.openzeppelin.com/reentrancy-after-istanbul/[Reentrancy After Istanbul].
  26 |     |  *
  27 |     |  * IMPORTANT: Deprecated. This storage-based reentrancy guard will be removed and replaced
  28 |     |  * by the {ReentrancyGuardTransient} variant in v6.0.
  29 |     |  *
  30 |     |  * @custom:stateless
  31 |     |  */
  32 |     | abstract contract ReentrancyGuard {
  33 |     |     using StorageSlot for bytes32;
  34 |     | 
  35 |     |     // keccak256(abi.encode(uint256(keccak256("openzeppelin.storage.ReentrancyGuard")) - 1)) & ~bytes32(uint256(0xff))
  36 |     |     bytes32 private constant REENTRANCY_GUARD_STORAGE =
  37 | *   |         0x9b779b17422d0df92223018b32b4d1fa46e071723d6817e2486d003becc55f00;
  38 |     | 
  39 |     |     // Booleans are more expensive than uint256 or any type that takes up a full
  40 |     |     // word because each write operation emits an extra SLOAD to first read the
  41 |     |     // slot's contents, replace the bits taken up by the boolean, and then write
  42 |     |     // back. This is the compiler's defense against contract upgrades and
  43 |     |     // pointer aliasing, and it cannot be disabled.
  44 |     | 
  45 |     |     // The values being non-zero value makes deployment a bit more expensive,
  46 |     |     // but in exchange the refund on every call to nonReentrant will be lower in
  47 |     |     // amount. Since refunds are capped to a percentage of the total
  48 |     |     // transaction's gas, it is best to keep them low in cases like this one, to
  49 |     |     // increase the likelihood of the full refund coming into effect.
  50 | *   |     uint256 private constant NOT_ENTERED = 1;
  51 |     |     uint256 private constant ENTERED = 2;
  52 |     | 
  53 |     |     /**
  54 |     |      * @dev Unauthorized reentrant call.
  55 |     |      */
  56 |     |     error ReentrancyGuardReentrantCall();
  57 |     | 
  58 |     |     constructor() {
  59 |     |         _reentrancyGuardStorageSlot().getUint256Slot().value = NOT_ENTERED;
  60 |     |     }
  61 |     | 
  62 |     |     /**
  63 |     |      * @dev Prevents a contract from calling itself, directly or indirectly.
  64 |     |      * Calling a `nonReentrant` function from another `nonReentrant`
  65 |     |      * function is not supported. It is possible to prevent this from happening
  66 |     |      * by making the `nonReentrant` function external, and making it call a
  67 |     |      * `private` function that does the actual work.
  68 |     |      */
  69 |     |     modifier nonReentrant() {
  70 |     |         _nonReentrantBefore();
  71 |     |         _;
  72 |     |         _nonReentrantAfter();
  73 |     |     }
  74 |     | 
  75 |     |     /**
  76 |     |      * @dev A `view` only version of {nonReentrant}. Use to block view functions
  77 |     |      * from being called, preventing reading from inconsistent contract state.
  78 |     |      *
  79 |     |      * CAUTION: This is a "view" modifier and does not change the reentrancy
  80 |     |      * status. Use it only on view functions. For payable or non-payable functions,
  81 |     |      * use the standard {nonReentrant} modifier instead.
  82 |     |      */
  83 |     |     modifier nonReentrantView() {
  84 |     |         _nonReentrantBeforeView();
  85 |     |         _;
  86 |     |     }
  87 |     | 
  88 |     |     function _nonReentrantBeforeView() private view {
  89 |     |         if (_reentrancyGuardEntered()) {
  90 |     |             revert ReentrancyGuardReentrantCall();
  91 |     |         }
  92 |     |     }
  93 |     | 
  94 |     |     function _nonReentrantBefore() private {
  95 |     |         // On the first call to nonReentrant, _status will be NOT_ENTERED
  96 |     |         _nonReentrantBeforeView();
  97 |     | 
  98 |     |         // Any calls to nonReentrant after this point will fail
  99 |     |         _reentrancyGuardStorageSlot().getUint256Slot().value = ENTERED;
 100 |     |     }
 101 |     | 
 102 |     |     function _nonReentrantAfter() private {
 103 |     |         // By storing the original value once again, a refund is triggered (see
 104 |     |         // https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-2200)
 105 |     |         _reentrancyGuardStorageSlot().getUint256Slot().value = NOT_ENTERED;
 106 |     |     }
 107 |     | 
 108 |     |     /**
 109 |     |      * @dev Returns true if the reentrancy guard is currently set to "entered", which indicates there is a
 110 |     |      * `nonReentrant` function in the call stack.
 111 |     |      */
 112 |     |     function _reentrancyGuardEntered() internal view returns (bool) {
 113 |     |         return _reentrancyGuardStorageSlot().getUint256Slot().value == ENTERED;
 114 |     |     }
 115 |     | 
 116 |     |     function _reentrancyGuardStorageSlot() internal pure virtual returns (bytes32) {
 117 |     |         return REENTRANCY_GUARD_STORAGE;
 118 |     |     }
 119 |     | }
 120 |     |
